Pre-clinical chapters

Chapter-level breakdown for each pre-clinical subject — use these as the unit of mapping when building competencies above.

Anatomy
  1. Introduction to Anatomy & Anatomical Terminology
    • Define anatomical position and use standard directional terms (superior, inferior, medial, lateral, proximal, distal) correctly.
    • Describe the anatomical planes of the body (sagittal, coronal, transverse) and their clinical relevance.
    • Classify the branches of anatomy (gross, microscopic, developmental, radiological) and their scope.
    • Explain basic movement terminology (flexion, extension, abduction, adduction, rotation).
    • Identify the major body cavities and their contents.
  2. Upper Limb — Osteology, Muscles, Nerves & Vessels
    • Identify the bones of the upper limb and their palpable surface landmarks.
    • Describe the brachial plexus formation and trace its major branches.
    • Explain the compartments of the arm and forearm and the muscles within each.
    • Correlate common nerve injuries (e.g., radial, ulnar, median) with resulting motor and sensory deficits.
    • Trace the arterial supply and venous drainage of the upper limb.
  3. Lower Limb — Osteology, Muscles, Nerves & Vessels
    • Identify the bones of the lower limb and their key osteological features.
    • Describe the formation of the lumbosacral plexus and its major branches.
    • Explain the fascial compartments of the thigh and leg and their muscle contents.
    • Correlate common nerve and vascular injuries with resulting clinical deficits (e.g., foot drop, claudication).
    • Describe the arches of the foot and their functional significance in gait.
  4. Thorax — Chest Wall, Mediastinum, Lungs & Heart
    • Describe the bony and muscular components of the thoracic wall and mechanics of respiration.
    • Identify the boundaries and contents of the mediastinum.
    • Describe the gross anatomy, lobes, and bronchopulmonary segments of the lungs.
    • Trace the chambers, valves, and conducting system of the heart.
    • Correlate surface landmarks with underlying thoracic viscera for clinical examination.
  5. Abdomen — Wall, Peritoneum & Viscera
    • Describe the layers of the anterior abdominal wall and clinically relevant regions/quadrants.
    • Explain the peritoneal reflections, mesenteries, and their relation to abdominal viscera.
    • Trace the gross anatomy and relations of the stomach, liver, and intestines.
    • Describe the blood supply and lymphatic drainage of the gastrointestinal tract.
    • Identify common sites of herniation and their anatomical basis.
  6. Pelvis & Perineum
    • Describe the bony pelvis, pelvic diaphragm, and differences between male and female pelvis.
    • Identify the pelvic viscera and their peritoneal relations.
    • Describe the boundaries and contents of the perineum, including the urogenital and anal triangles.
    • Trace the blood supply, innervation, and lymphatic drainage of pelvic organs.
    • Explain the clinical significance of pelvic diameters in obstetric practice.
  7. Head & Neck
    • Identify the bones of the skull and describe the cranial fossae and their contents.
    • Trace the course and distribution of the cranial nerves.
    • Describe the triangles of the neck and their contents.
    • Explain the anatomy of the pharynx, larynx, and thyroid gland.
    • Correlate facial and scalp anatomy with common clinical presentations (e.g., facial nerve palsy).
  8. Neuroanatomy — Brain, Spinal Cord & Pathways
    • Describe the gross organization of the brain, brainstem, cerebellum, and spinal cord.
    • Trace major ascending (sensory) and descending (motor) pathways.
    • Describe the blood supply of the brain and the clinical basis of stroke syndromes.
    • Explain the ventricular system and cerebrospinal fluid circulation.
    • Correlate lesions at various neuroaxis levels with resulting clinical deficits.
  9. Special Senses (Eye & Ear)
    • Describe the gross anatomy of the eyeball and its accessory structures.
    • Explain the anatomical basis of the visual pathway and pupillary reflexes.
    • Describe the external, middle, and inner ear and their functional anatomy.
    • Trace the auditory and vestibular pathways.
    • Correlate common lesions of the eye and ear with their anatomical basis.
  10. General & Systemic Histology
    • Identify the four basic tissue types and their subtypes under the microscope.
    • Describe the microscopic structure of major organs across body systems.
    • Correlate histological structure with organ function.
    • Differentiate normal histology from basic pathological changes.
    • Use histological terminology accurately in describing tissue sections.
  11. General Embryology
    • Describe the stages of fertilization, cleavage, and implantation.
    • Explain gastrulation and the formation of the three germ layers.
    • Describe the formation of the neural tube and somites.
    • Explain the development and functions of the placenta and fetal membranes.
    • Identify critical periods of development and their relevance to teratogenesis.
  12. Systemic Embryology & Congenital Anomalies
    • Describe the embryological development of the cardiovascular, respiratory, and gastrointestinal systems.
    • Explain the development of the urogenital system and common anomalies.
    • Describe limb and craniofacial development and related congenital defects.
    • Correlate faulty development with clinically important congenital malformations.
    • Explain the embryological basis of common pediatric surgical conditions.
  13. Genetics & Genomic Basis of Disease
    • Describe the basic principles of Mendelian and non-Mendelian inheritance.
    • Explain chromosomal structure and common numerical/structural abnormalities.
    • Interpret a basic pedigree chart for inheritance patterns.
    • Describe the genetic basis of common inherited disorders.
    • Explain the principles and clinical applications of genetic counseling.
  14. Surface, Radiological & Cross-Sectional Anatomy
    • Identify surface landmarks corresponding to underlying organs and structures.
    • Interpret normal anatomy on plain radiographs of the chest, abdomen, and limbs.
    • Identify key anatomical structures on cross-sectional CT and MRI images.
    • Correlate cross-sectional imaging anatomy with clinical examination findings.
    • Apply surface anatomy knowledge to common clinical and procedural skills (e.g., venipuncture, lumbar puncture sites).
Physiology
  1. General Physiology & Cell Physiology
    • Describe the structure and function of the cell membrane and transport mechanisms across it.
    • Explain the resting membrane potential and its ionic basis.
    • Describe the mechanisms of cell signaling and receptor physiology.
    • Explain homeostasis and feedback control mechanisms in the body.
    • Describe the basic principles of body fluid compartments.
  2. Blood & Immunity
    • Describe the composition and functions of blood and plasma proteins.
    • Explain erythropoiesis, hemoglobin synthesis, and the anemia classification.
    • Describe the mechanism of hemostasis and blood coagulation.
    • Explain the basis of ABO and Rh blood grouping and transfusion reactions.
    • Describe the physiological basis of innate and acquired immunity.
  3. Nerve-Muscle Physiology
    • Explain the generation and propagation of the nerve action potential.
    • Describe the structure and function of the neuromuscular junction.
    • Explain the molecular basis of skeletal muscle contraction.
    • Differentiate the physiological properties of skeletal, cardiac, and smooth muscle.
    • Describe the classification and physiology of nerve fibers.
  4. Central Nervous System
    • Describe the physiological organization of the cerebral cortex and its functional areas.
    • Explain the physiology of reflexes and motor control pathways.
    • Describe the physiology of sleep and its stages.
    • Explain the mechanisms underlying learning and memory.
    • Describe the physiological basis of pain sensation and modulation.
  5. Autonomic Nervous System
    • Differentiate the anatomical and functional organization of the sympathetic and parasympathetic systems.
    • Describe the neurotransmitters and receptors involved in autonomic function.
    • Explain the autonomic control of cardiovascular, respiratory, and GI functions.
    • Describe the physiological basis of the fight-or-flight response.
    • Explain common autonomic dysfunctions and their physiological basis.
  6. Special Senses
    • Explain the physiology of phototransduction and visual processing.
    • Describe the physiology of sound transduction and auditory pathways.
    • Explain the physiological basis of taste and olfaction.
    • Describe the physiology of vestibular function and balance.
    • Explain the mechanisms of accommodation and pupillary reflexes.
  7. Cardiovascular System
    • Describe the cardiac cycle and its correlation with pressure, volume, and ECG changes.
    • Explain the origin and conduction of the cardiac impulse.
    • Describe the regulation of blood pressure and cardiac output.
    • Explain the physiological basis of the ECG and common arrhythmias.
    • Describe the regional circulations (coronary, cerebral, and peripheral).
  8. Respiratory System
    • Describe the mechanics of breathing and lung compliance.
    • Explain gas exchange and the transport of oxygen and carbon dioxide in blood.
    • Describe the neural and chemical regulation of respiration.
    • Interpret spirometry findings and common lung volumes/capacities.
    • Explain the physiological basis of hypoxia and respiratory failure.
  9. Renal Physiology & Body Fluids
    • Describe the process of glomerular filtration and its regulation.
    • Explain tubular reabsorption and secretion mechanisms along the nephron.
    • Describe the renal mechanisms of urine concentration and dilution.
    • Explain the role of the kidney in acid-base regulation.
    • Describe the regulation of body fluid volume and osmolarity.
  10. Gastrointestinal System
    • Describe the physiology of salivary, gastric, and pancreatic secretions.
    • Explain the mechanisms of digestion and absorption of nutrients.
    • Describe gastrointestinal motility and its neural/hormonal control.
    • Explain the physiological functions of the liver and biliary system.
    • Describe the gut hormones and their regulatory roles.
  11. Endocrine System
    • Describe the mechanisms of hormone synthesis, transport, and action.
    • Explain the hypothalamo-pituitary axis and its feedback regulation.
    • Describe the physiology of the thyroid, parathyroid, and adrenal glands.
    • Explain the physiological regulation of blood glucose by insulin and glucagon.
    • Describe the physiological basis of common endocrine disorders.
  12. Reproductive System
    • Describe the physiology of spermatogenesis and male reproductive hormones.
    • Explain the ovarian and menstrual cycle and their hormonal regulation.
    • Describe the physiological changes during pregnancy and parturition.
    • Explain the physiology of lactation.
    • Describe the hormonal basis of puberty and menopause.
  13. Integrated & Exercise Physiology
    • Describe the physiological changes in the cardiovascular and respiratory systems during exercise.
    • Explain the concept of homeostasis under stress and adaptive responses.
    • Describe the physiology of thermoregulation.
    • Explain the physiological basis of acclimatization to high altitude.
    • Describe the integrated response of multiple organ systems during physical exertion.
Biochemistry
  1. Biomolecules — Carbohydrates, Lipids & Proteins
    • Classify carbohydrates, lipids, and proteins based on their chemical structure.
    • Describe the structural organization of proteins (primary to quaternary).
    • Explain the classification and biological roles of lipids.
    • Describe the structure and function of biological membranes.
    • Explain the chemistry of glycoproteins and proteoglycans.
  2. Enzymes & Enzyme Kinetics
    • Describe enzyme classification and mechanism of catalysis.
    • Explain Michaelis-Menten kinetics and factors affecting enzyme activity.
    • Describe the types and mechanisms of enzyme inhibition.
    • Explain the regulation of enzyme activity (allosteric, covalent modification).
    • Describe the clinical significance of plasma enzymes as diagnostic markers.
  3. Carbohydrate Metabolism
    • Describe the steps and regulation of glycolysis.
    • Explain the citric acid cycle and its regulatory control points.
    • Describe glycogen synthesis and breakdown and their hormonal regulation.
    • Explain gluconeogenesis and its role in maintaining blood glucose.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of common carbohydrate metabolic disorders.
  4. Lipid Metabolism
    • Describe the pathway of fatty acid synthesis and beta-oxidation.
    • Explain ketone body formation and its clinical relevance.
    • Describe cholesterol synthesis, transport, and regulation.
    • Explain lipoprotein metabolism and its role in atherosclerosis.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of common lipid disorders.
  5. Protein & Amino Acid Metabolism
    • Describe the pathways of amino acid catabolism and transamination.
    • Explain the urea cycle and its role in ammonia detoxification.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of inborn errors of amino acid metabolism.
    • Explain the synthesis and significance of biologically important amino acid derivatives.
    • Describe protein turnover and nitrogen balance.
  6. Nucleic Acid Metabolism & Molecular Biology
    • Describe the structure and organization of DNA and RNA.
    • Explain the processes of DNA replication, transcription, and translation.
    • Describe purine and pyrimidine metabolism and related disorders (e.g., gout).
    • Explain the principles of gene regulation and mutation.
    • Describe common recombinant DNA techniques and their clinical applications.
  7. Vitamins & Minerals
    • Classify vitamins as fat-soluble or water-soluble with their sources and functions.
    • Describe the biochemical role of vitamins as coenzymes.
    • Explain the clinical features of common vitamin deficiency disorders.
    • Describe the physiological roles of major and trace minerals.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of mineral deficiency and toxicity states.
  8. Nutrition & Energy Metabolism
    • Describe the concept of balanced diet and daily energy requirements.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of protein-energy malnutrition.
    • Describe the concept of basal metabolic rate and its determinants.
    • Explain the integration of carbohydrate, lipid, and protein metabolism in the fed and fasting states.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of obesity and metabolic syndrome.
  9. Hormones & Endocrine Biochemistry
    • Classify hormones based on chemical nature and mechanism of action.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of hormone-receptor interactions and signal transduction.
    • Explain the biochemistry of second messenger systems.
    • Describe the biochemical regulation of blood glucose by hormones.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of common endocrine disorders.
  10. Organ Function Tests (Liver, Kidney, Thyroid)
    • Interpret common liver function tests and their clinical significance.
    • Interpret renal function tests including creatinine clearance and GFR estimation.
    • Describe thyroid function tests and their interpretation.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of jaundice and its classification.
    • Describe the biochemical basis of common organ-specific diagnostic panels.
  11. Acid-Base Balance & Water-Electrolyte Homeostasis
    • Describe the buffer systems maintaining blood pH.
    • Explain the renal and respiratory compensation mechanisms in acid-base disorders.
    • Classify and interpret common acid-base disorders using arterial blood gas values.
    • Describe the regulation of water and electrolyte balance.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of common electrolyte imbalances.
  12. Immunology
    • Describe the structure and function of immunoglobulins.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of antigen-antibody interactions.
    • Describe the complement system and its activation pathways.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of hypersensitivity reactions.
    • Describe common immunological laboratory techniques (ELISA, Western blot).
  13. Cancer Biology & Molecular Genetics
    • Describe the molecular basis of oncogenes and tumor suppressor genes.
    • Explain the biochemical hallmarks of cancer cells.
    • Describe the role of tumor markers in diagnosis and monitoring.
    • Explain the molecular basis of carcinogenesis.
    • Describe the principles of molecular diagnostic techniques used in cancer detection.
  14. Instrumentation & Clinical/Diagnostic Biochemistry
    • Describe the principles of spectrophotometry and its clinical applications.
    • Explain the principles of chromatography and electrophoresis techniques.
    • Describe the principles of centrifugation and its diagnostic uses.
    • Explain quality control measures in a clinical biochemistry laboratory.
    • Describe the principles of automated analyzers used in diagnostic biochemistry.

Para-clinical chapters

Chapter-level breakdown for each para-clinical subject — use these as the unit of mapping when building competencies above.

Pathology
  1. General Pathology — Cell Injury & Adaptation
    • Describe the causes and mechanisms of reversible and irreversible cell injury.
    • Explain the morphological patterns of cellular adaptation (atrophy, hypertrophy, hyperplasia, metaplasia).
    • Describe the mechanisms and morphology of necrosis and apoptosis.
    • Explain the biochemical basis of free radical injury.
    • Correlate cell injury mechanisms with common clinical disease processes.
  2. Inflammation & Repair
    • Describe the vascular and cellular events of acute inflammation.
    • Explain the chemical mediators involved in inflammation.
    • Differentiate the morphological patterns of acute and chronic inflammation.
    • Describe the process of wound healing and factors affecting it.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of granulomatous inflammation.
  3. Hemodynamic Disorders (Thrombosis, Embolism, Shock)
    • Describe the pathogenesis of edema and its classification.
    • Explain Virchow's triad and the pathogenesis of thrombosis.
    • Differentiate types of embolism and their clinical consequences.
    • Describe the stages and pathophysiology of shock.
    • Explain the mechanisms of infarction and factors influencing its outcome.
  4. Immunopathology
    • Classify the types of hypersensitivity reactions with examples.
    • Describe the pathogenesis of autoimmune diseases.
    • Explain the pathology of transplant rejection.
    • Describe the pathological basis of immunodeficiency disorders.
    • Correlate immunopathological mechanisms with common systemic diseases.
  5. Neoplasia — General Principles
    • Define and classify neoplasms based on behavior and tissue origin.
    • Describe the molecular basis of carcinogenesis.
    • Differentiate the characteristics of benign and malignant tumors.
    • Explain the mechanisms of tumor invasion and metastasis.
    • Describe the grading and staging systems used in oncology.
  6. Genetic & Pediatric Disorders
    • Describe the pathological basis of chromosomal disorders.
    • Explain the mechanisms of single-gene inherited disorders.
    • Describe common pediatric tumors and developmental pathologies.
    • Explain the pathology of intrauterine growth disorders.
    • Correlate genetic mechanisms with common congenital syndromes.
  7. Hematology & Hematopoietic Disorders
    • Classify anemias based on morphology and pathogenesis.
    • Describe the pathology of leukemias and lymphomas.
    • Explain the pathophysiology of bleeding and coagulation disorders.
    • Describe the pathology of myeloproliferative disorders.
    • Interpret peripheral blood smear and bone marrow findings in common hematologic disorders.
  8. Cardiovascular Pathology
    • Describe the pathogenesis of atherosclerosis and its complications.
    • Explain the pathology of ischemic heart disease.
    • Describe the pathology of hypertensive and valvular heart disease.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of cardiomyopathies and myocarditis.
    • Describe the pathology of congenital heart diseases.
  9. Respiratory Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of obstructive and restrictive lung diseases.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of pneumonia and lung abscess.
    • Describe the pathology of pulmonary tuberculosis.
    • Explain the pathogenesis and classification of lung tumors.
    • Describe the pathology of pulmonary vascular disorders.
  10. Gastrointestinal & Hepatobiliary Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of gastritis and peptic ulcer disease.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of inflammatory bowel disease.
    • Describe the pathology of viral and other forms of hepatitis.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of cirrhosis and its complications.
    • Describe the pathology of common gastrointestinal and hepatobiliary tumors.
  11. Renal & Urinary Tract Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of glomerular diseases.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of acute and chronic kidney injury.
    • Describe the pathology of tubulointerstitial diseases.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of urinary tract calculi and obstruction.
    • Describe the pathology of renal and bladder tumors.
  12. Male & Female Genital Tract Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of common lesions of the cervix, uterus, and ovary.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of gestational trophoblastic disease.
    • Describe the pathology of prostate and testicular disorders.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of common sexually transmitted infections' tissue changes.
    • Correlate genital tract pathology with screening and diagnostic methods (e.g., Pap smear).
  13. Breast Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of benign breast diseases.
    • Explain the classification and pathogenesis of breast carcinoma.
    • Describe the role of biomarkers in breast cancer diagnosis and prognosis.
    • Explain the pathological basis of breast cancer screening.
    • Correlate breast pathology findings with clinical staging.
  14. Endocrine Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of thyroid disorders including thyroiditis and neoplasms.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of diabetes mellitus and its complications.
    • Describe the pathology of pituitary and adrenal disorders.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of parathyroid disorders.
    • Describe the pathology of neuroendocrine tumors.
  15. Musculoskeletal & Soft Tissue Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of metabolic bone diseases.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of osteomyelitis and bone tumors.
    • Describe the pathology of degenerative and inflammatory joint diseases.
    • Explain the classification of soft tissue tumors.
    • Correlate musculoskeletal pathology with imaging and biopsy findings.
  16. Nervous System Pathology
    • Describe the pathology of common CNS infections.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of cerebrovascular disease.
    • Describe the pathology of degenerative and demyelinating CNS disorders.
    • Explain the classification of CNS tumors.
    • Describe the pathology of traumatic brain and spinal cord injury.
  17. Clinical Pathology & Laboratory Methods
    • Describe the principles of specimen collection and processing for histopathology and cytology.
    • Explain common special stains and their diagnostic applications.
    • Describe the principles of immunohistochemistry.
    • Explain quality assurance practices in a pathology laboratory.
    • Interpret basic cytological and biopsy reports.
Microbiology
  1. General Microbiology & Bacterial Morphology
    • Describe the structure and classification of bacteria.
    • Explain bacterial growth, nutrition, and reproduction.
    • Describe bacterial genetics and mechanisms of gene transfer.
    • Explain the principles of Gram staining and other bacteriological stains.
    • Describe the normal microbial flora of the human body.
  2. Sterilization, Disinfection & Lab Diagnosis
    • Describe the physical and chemical methods of sterilization and disinfection.
    • Explain the principles of culture media and bacterial identification.
    • Describe the methods of antimicrobial susceptibility testing.
    • Explain the principles of specimen collection and transport for microbiological diagnosis.
    • Describe biosafety levels and laboratory safety practices.
  3. Immunology & Immunological Techniques
    • Describe the components and functions of innate and adaptive immunity.
    • Explain the structure and function of antibodies.
    • Describe the principles of common serological and immunological tests.
    • Explain the mechanism of vaccine-induced immunity.
    • Describe the immunological basis of hypersensitivity reactions.
  4. Systematic Bacteriology — Gram-Positive Cocci & Bacilli
    • Describe the pathogenesis and laboratory diagnosis of Staphylococcus and Streptococcus infections.
    • Explain the virulence factors of common gram-positive bacilli (e.g., Corynebacterium, Bacillus, Clostridium).
    • Describe the clinical manifestations of gram-positive bacterial infections.
    • Explain the antimicrobial susceptibility patterns of major gram-positive pathogens.
    • Describe preventive and control measures for gram-positive bacterial infections.
  5. Systematic Bacteriology — Gram-Negative Organisms
    • Describe the pathogenesis of Enterobacteriaceae infections.
    • Explain the microbiology and pathogenesis of Vibrio, Salmonella, and Shigella.
    • Describe the pathogenesis of Neisseria and Haemophilus infections.
    • Explain the laboratory diagnosis of common gram-negative bacterial infections.
    • Describe antimicrobial resistance mechanisms in gram-negative organisms.
  6. Mycobacteria & Spirochetes
    • Describe the microbiology and pathogenesis of Mycobacterium tuberculosis.
    • Explain the laboratory diagnosis of tuberculosis and leprosy.
    • Describe the microbiology and pathogenesis of Treponema, Leptospira, and Borrelia.
    • Explain the clinical stages and diagnosis of syphilis.
    • Describe treatment principles for mycobacterial and spirochetal infections.
  7. General & Systematic Virology
    • Describe the structure, classification, and replication of viruses.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of common DNA and RNA viral infections.
    • Describe the laboratory diagnosis of viral infections.
    • Explain the principles of antiviral therapy.
    • Describe the epidemiology and prevention of common viral diseases.
  8. HIV & Retroviruses
    • Describe the structure and life cycle of HIV.
    • Explain the pathogenesis and clinical stages of HIV infection.
    • Describe the laboratory diagnosis and monitoring of HIV infection.
    • Explain the principles of antiretroviral therapy.
    • Describe strategies for prevention and control of HIV transmission.
  9. Medical Mycology
    • Classify fungi of medical importance based on morphology.
    • Describe the pathogenesis of superficial, subcutaneous, and systemic mycoses.
    • Explain the laboratory diagnosis of fungal infections.
    • Describe opportunistic fungal infections in immunocompromised patients.
    • Explain the principles of antifungal therapy.
  10. Medical Parasitology — Protozoa
    • Describe the life cycle and pathogenesis of Plasmodium species.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of amoebiasis and giardiasis.
    • Describe the laboratory diagnosis of common protozoal infections.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of Leishmania and Trypanosoma infections.
    • Describe preventive measures for protozoal diseases.
  11. Medical Parasitology — Helminths
    • Describe the life cycle and pathogenesis of common intestinal nematodes.
    • Explain the pathogenesis of cestode and trematode infections.
    • Describe the laboratory diagnosis of helminthic infections.
    • Explain the clinical manifestations of filarial infections.
    • Describe control and prevention strategies for soil-transmitted helminths.
  12. Medical Entomology
    • Classify arthropods of medical importance.
    • Describe the role of vectors in disease transmission.
    • Explain the life cycle of mosquitoes and their role in disease transmission.
    • Describe methods of vector control.
    • Explain the epidemiological significance of arthropod-borne diseases.
  13. Hospital Infection Control & Biomedical Waste
    • Describe the principles of hospital infection prevention and control.
    • Explain the classification and management of biomedical waste.
    • Describe standard precautions and personal protective equipment use.
    • Explain the surveillance methods for healthcare-associated infections.
    • Describe outbreak investigation principles in a hospital setting.
  14. Applied & Diagnostic Microbiology
    • Describe the principles of molecular diagnostic techniques (PCR) in microbiology.
    • Explain the role of automated culture and identification systems.
    • Describe the interpretation of common microbiological reports.
    • Explain the principles of antimicrobial stewardship.
    • Describe emerging and re-emerging infectious disease threats.
Pharmacology
  1. General Pharmacology — Pharmacokinetics
    • Describe the processes of drug absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion.
    • Explain the concepts of bioavailability and first-pass metabolism.
    • Describe the factors affecting drug distribution and plasma protein binding.
    • Explain the principles of drug half-life and steady-state concentration.
    • Describe routes of drug administration and their pharmacokinetic implications.
  2. General Pharmacology — Pharmacodynamics
    • Describe the mechanisms of drug-receptor interactions.
    • Explain the concepts of agonists, antagonists, and partial agonists.
    • Describe dose-response relationships and therapeutic index.
    • Explain mechanisms of drug tolerance and dependence.
    • Describe the principles of adverse drug reactions and drug interactions.
  3. Autonomic Nervous System Pharmacology
    • Describe the pharmacology of cholinergic agonists and antagonists.
    • Explain the pharmacology of adrenergic agonists and antagonists.
    • Describe the clinical uses of autonomic drugs.
    • Explain the mechanism of neuromuscular blocking agents.
    • Describe the toxicology and management of autonomic drug overdose.
  4. Autacoids & Related Drugs
    • Describe the pharmacology of histamine and antihistamines.
    • Explain the pharmacology of serotonin and its antagonists.
    • Describe the pharmacology of prostaglandins and related eicosanoids.
    • Explain the clinical applications of autacoid-related drugs.
    • Describe the pharmacology of ergot alkaloids.
  5. Cardiovascular Pharmacology
    • Describe the pharmacology of antihypertensive drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of antianginal and antiarrhythmic drugs.
    • Describe the pharmacology of drugs used in heart failure.
    • Explain the pharmacology of diuretics.
    • Describe the pharmacology of lipid-lowering agents.
  6. Drugs Acting on Blood & Hematopoietic System
    • Describe the pharmacology of anticoagulants and antiplatelet agents.
    • Explain the pharmacology of fibrinolytic drugs.
    • Describe the pharmacology of drugs used to treat anemia.
    • Explain the pharmacology of hematopoietic growth factors.
    • Describe the clinical monitoring required for anticoagulant therapy.
  7. Respiratory System Pharmacology
    • Describe the pharmacology of bronchodilators.
    • Explain the pharmacology of anti-inflammatory drugs used in asthma and COPD.
    • Describe the pharmacology of antitussives and expectorants.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs used in pulmonary hypertension.
    • Describe the clinical use of inhalational drug delivery systems.
  8. Gastrointestinal Pharmacology
    • Describe the pharmacology of antacids and anti-secretory drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of antiemetics and prokinetic agents.
    • Describe the pharmacology of laxatives and antidiarrheal drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs used in inflammatory bowel disease.
    • Describe the pharmacology of drugs used to treat H. pylori infection.
  9. Central Nervous System Pharmacology
    • Describe the pharmacology of general and local anesthetics.
    • Explain the pharmacology of sedative-hypnotics and anxiolytics.
    • Describe the pharmacology of antiepileptic drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of antipsychotics and antidepressants.
    • Describe the pharmacology of opioid analgesics and their antagonists.
  10. Autocoids, NSAIDs & Anti-inflammatory Drugs
    • Describe the mechanism of action of NSAIDs and their adverse effects.
    • Explain the pharmacology of corticosteroids.
    • Describe the pharmacology of disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs used in gout.
    • Describe the clinical use of COX-2 selective inhibitors.
  11. Endocrine Pharmacology (Hormones & Antidiabetics)
    • Describe the pharmacology of insulin and oral hypoglycemic agents.
    • Explain the pharmacology of thyroid and antithyroid drugs.
    • Describe the pharmacology of corticosteroids and sex hormones.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs affecting bone mineral metabolism.
    • Describe the pharmacology of pituitary hormone analogs.
  12. Chemotherapy — Antimicrobials
    • Describe the classification and mechanism of action of antibacterial drugs.
    • Explain the principles of rational antibiotic use.
    • Describe the mechanisms of antimicrobial resistance.
    • Explain the pharmacology of beta-lactam and other major antibiotic classes.
    • Describe the adverse effects and monitoring of common antibiotics.
  13. Chemotherapy — Antivirals, Antifungals & Antiparasitics
    • Describe the pharmacology of antiviral drugs including antiretrovirals.
    • Explain the pharmacology of antifungal agents.
    • Describe the pharmacology of antimalarial and antiprotozoal drugs.
    • Explain the pharmacology of anthelmintic drugs.
    • Describe drug resistance mechanisms in antiparasitic therapy.
  14. Anticancer Chemotherapy
    • Describe the classification and mechanism of action of anticancer drugs.
    • Explain the principles of combination chemotherapy.
    • Describe the adverse effects of common chemotherapeutic agents.
    • Explain the pharmacology of targeted and immunotherapy agents.
    • Describe the principles of cell-cycle-specific and non-specific drugs.
  15. Chemotherapy of Tuberculosis & Leprosy
    • Describe the first-line and second-line drugs used in tuberculosis treatment.
    • Explain the principles of directly observed treatment short-course (DOTS).
    • Describe the pharmacology of multidrug therapy for leprosy.
    • Explain the management of drug-resistant tuberculosis.
    • Describe the adverse effects and monitoring of anti-tubercular drugs.
  16. Immunopharmacology & Vaccines
    • Describe the pharmacology of immunosuppressive drugs.
    • Explain the mechanism of action of biologic and monoclonal antibody therapies.
    • Describe the principles of vaccine development and immunization schedules.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs used in organ transplantation.
    • Describe adverse events following immunization and their management.
  17. Vitamins, Minerals & Miscellaneous Agents
    • Describe the therapeutic uses of vitamin and mineral supplements.
    • Explain the pharmacology of drugs used in nutritional deficiency states.
    • Describe the pharmacology of chelating agents.
    • Explain the clinical uses of enzyme preparations and miscellaneous agents.
    • Describe drug interactions involving vitamins and minerals.
  18. Principles of Toxicology & Poisoning
    • Describe the general principles of management of acute poisoning.
    • Explain the pharmacology of common antidotes.
    • Describe methods to reduce absorption and enhance elimination of poisons.
    • Explain the clinical features and management of common drug overdoses.
    • Describe the principles of poison information and prevention.
Forensic Medicine & Toxicology
  1. Introduction to Forensic Medicine & Legal Procedures
    • Describe the scope and branches of forensic medicine.
    • Explain the legal procedures involved in medicolegal examination.
    • Describe the role of a doctor as an expert witness in court.
    • Explain the classification of inquests and their procedures.
    • Describe types of medicolegal documents and their importance.
  2. Identification (Age, Sex, Stature, Fingerprints)
    • Describe the medicolegal methods of age estimation.
    • Explain the methods used to determine sex from skeletal remains.
    • Describe the methods of stature estimation from bones.
    • Explain the principles and significance of fingerprint identification.
    • Describe the use of DNA and other modern techniques in personal identification.
  3. Death — Definition, Changes & Postmortem Interval
    • Define somatic and cellular death and describe modes of death.
    • Describe the early and late postmortem changes.
    • Explain the methods used to estimate the time since death.
    • Describe the medicolegal significance of postmortem lividity and rigor mortis.
    • Explain the factors affecting the rate of decomposition.
  4. Medicolegal Autopsy & Exhumation
    • Describe the indications and procedure of a medicolegal autopsy.
    • Explain the technique of organ examination during autopsy.
    • Describe the procedure and legal requirements for exhumation.
    • Explain the precautions to be taken during a medicolegal autopsy.
    • Describe the preparation of a postmortem report.
  5. Injuries — Mechanical, Thermal & Firearm
    • Classify wounds based on the mechanism of injury.
    • Describe the characteristics of injuries caused by blunt and sharp force.
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of thermal injuries (burns and scalds).
    • Describe the characteristics of firearm wounds and their medicolegal significance.
    • Explain the age estimation of wounds.
  6. Asphyxial Deaths
    • Classify the types of asphyxial deaths.
    • Describe the postmortem findings in hanging and strangulation.
    • Explain the medicolegal differentiation of suicidal, homicidal, and accidental asphyxia.
    • Describe the findings in drowning deaths.
    • Explain the mechanism and findings in traumatic and smothering asphyxia.
  7. Sexual Offences & Medical Jurisprudence of Sex
    • Describe the medicolegal examination in cases of sexual assault.
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of virginity and pregnancy determination.
    • Describe the legal definitions and examination protocols for sexual offences.
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of impotence and sterility.
    • Describe the collection and preservation of forensic evidence in sexual offence cases.
  8. Forensic Psychiatry
    • Describe the legal definitions of unsoundness of mind.
    • Explain the medicolegal responsibilities in criminal and civil cases involving mental illness.
    • Describe the concept of testamentary capacity.
    • Explain the procedure for certification of mental illness.
    • Describe the forensic psychiatric aspects of diminished responsibility.
  9. Medical Law, Ethics & Consent
    • Describe the principles of medical ethics and professional conduct.
    • Explain the legal requirements of informed consent.
    • Describe the doctrine of confidentiality and its exceptions.
    • Explain the legal aspects of euthanasia and end-of-life care.
    • Describe the regulatory bodies governing medical practice.
  10. Consumer Protection & Medical Negligence
    • Define medical negligence and describe its essential elements.
    • Explain the application of consumer protection law to medical practice.
    • Describe the concept of vicarious liability in medical negligence.
    • Explain the doctrine of res ipsa loquitur.
    • Describe strategies for the prevention of medical negligence claims.
  11. General Toxicology & Principles of Management
    • Describe the general principles of diagnosis and management of poisoning.
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of common poisoning cases.
    • Describe the methods of collection and preservation of viscera for chemical analysis.
    • Explain the general symptoms and signs of poisoning.
    • Describe the classification of poisons based on their action.
  12. Corrosive & Irritant Poisons
    • Describe the clinical features and management of corrosive acid and alkali poisoning.
    • Explain the pathological findings in corrosive poisoning.
    • Describe the clinical features of irritant poisons (e.g., arsenic, mercury).
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of irritant poison cases.
    • Describe the antidotal treatment for common corrosive and irritant poisons.
  13. Neurotoxic & Deliriant Poisons
    • Describe the clinical features and management of opioid poisoning.
    • Explain the clinical features of poisoning by CNS depressants and stimulants.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of deliriant plant poisoning (e.g., datura).
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of neurotoxic poison cases.
    • Describe the antidotal treatment for common neurotoxic poisons.
  14. Insecticides, Pesticides & Agrochemical Poisoning
    • Describe the mechanism and clinical features of organophosphorus poisoning.
    • Explain the management of organophosphorus and carbamate poisoning.
    • Describe the clinical features of aluminium phosphide poisoning.
    • Explain the medicolegal significance of agrochemical poisoning in rural settings.
    • Describe preventive measures for accidental pesticide poisoning.
  15. Drug Dependence & Deleterious Substances
    • Describe the clinical features of common substance use disorders.
    • Explain the medicolegal aspects of drug dependence and addiction.
    • Describe the effects and management of alcohol intoxication and withdrawal.
    • Explain the forensic aspects of common deleterious substances (e.g., solvents).
    • Describe the legal framework governing narcotic and psychotropic substances.
  16. Forensic Toxicology & DNA Fingerprinting
    • Describe the principles of forensic toxicological analysis.
    • Explain the chain of custody requirements for forensic samples.
    • Describe the principles and applications of DNA fingerprinting in forensic practice.
    • Explain the use of forensic toxicology in medicolegal death investigation.
    • Describe emerging techniques in forensic analytical toxicology.
Community Medicine (PSM)
  1. Concept of Health, Disease & Preventive Medicine
    • Define health and disease and describe the natural history of disease.
    • Explain the levels of prevention in preventive medicine.
    • Describe the concept of the iceberg phenomenon in disease.
    • Explain the determinants of health.
    • Describe the modes of intervention at different levels of prevention.
  2. Epidemiology — Principles & Methods
    • Describe the basic principles and uses of epidemiology.
    • Explain the measures of disease frequency and association.
    • Describe the types and design of epidemiological studies.
    • Explain the concept of bias and confounding in epidemiological studies.
    • Describe the criteria for establishing causation.
  3. Screening for Disease & Investigation of Epidemics
    • Describe the principles and criteria of screening for disease.
    • Explain the concepts of sensitivity, specificity, and predictive value.
    • Describe the steps involved in the investigation of an epidemic.
    • Explain the epidemic curve and its interpretation.
    • Describe the principles of outbreak control and containment.
  4. Demography & Family Planning
    • Describe the demographic cycle and vital statistics indicators.
    • Explain the methods of population data collection (census, registration).
    • Describe the various contraceptive methods and their mechanisms.
    • Explain the national family planning programme and its components.
    • Describe the concept of the demographic transition.
  5. Maternal & Child Health
    • Describe the components of antenatal, intranatal, and postnatal care.
    • Explain the indicators used to assess maternal and child health status.
    • Describe the national programmes related to maternal and child health.
    • Explain the concept of high-risk pregnancy and its management.
    • Describe strategies for reducing maternal and infant mortality.
  6. Nutrition & Nutritional Disorders
    • Describe the classification and assessment of nutritional status.
    • Explain the etiology and prevention of protein-energy malnutrition.
    • Describe the community-based management of micronutrient deficiency disorders.
    • Explain the national nutritional programmes and their objectives.
    • Describe methods of nutritional surveillance.
  7. Environmental Health & Sanitation
    • Describe the principles of safe water supply and water quality standards.
    • Explain the methods of excreta and solid waste disposal.
    • Describe the principles of housing and ventilation for health.
    • Explain the health hazards associated with air and water pollution.
    • Describe the principles of environmental sanitation in disaster situations.
  8. Occupational Health
    • Describe the common occupational hazards and their health effects.
    • Explain the principles of occupational disease prevention.
    • Describe the legislative framework for occupational health and safety.
    • Explain the concept of ergonomics in the workplace.
    • Describe the role of periodic medical examinations in occupational health.
  9. Communicable Diseases — Respiratory & Contact
    • Describe the epidemiology and control of tuberculosis.
    • Explain the epidemiology and prevention of respiratory viral infections.
    • Describe the epidemiology and control of leprosy.
    • Explain the modes of transmission and prevention of contact diseases.
    • Describe national programmes for control of respiratory and contact diseases.
  10. Communicable Diseases — Vector-Borne
    • Describe the epidemiology and control of malaria.
    • Explain the epidemiology and prevention of dengue and other arboviral diseases.
    • Describe the epidemiology and control of filariasis.
    • Explain integrated vector management strategies.
    • Describe national programmes for vector-borne disease control.
  11. Communicable Diseases — Water & Food-Borne
    • Describe the epidemiology and prevention of cholera and other diarrheal diseases.
    • Explain the epidemiology and prevention of typhoid fever.
    • Describe the epidemiology and prevention of viral hepatitis A and E.
    • Explain the principles of food safety and hygiene.
    • Describe outbreak investigation for water and food-borne diseases.
  12. Non-Communicable Diseases
    • Describe the epidemiology and risk factors of cardiovascular disease.
    • Explain the epidemiology and prevention of diabetes mellitus.
    • Describe the epidemiology and prevention of cancer.
    • Explain the national programme for prevention and control of NCDs.
    • Describe the concept of the double burden of disease.
  13. Immunization & National Health Programmes
    • Describe the national immunization schedule and cold chain system.
    • Explain the components of the Universal Immunization Programme.
    • Describe major national health programmes and their objectives.
    • Explain the concept of herd immunity.
    • Describe the surveillance systems for vaccine-preventable diseases.
  14. Health Education & Communication
    • Describe the principles and methods of health education.
    • Explain the models of health behavior change.
    • Describe the use of communication media in health promotion.
    • Explain the role of community participation in health education.
    • Describe strategies for effective interpersonal communication in healthcare.
  15. Biostatistics
    • Describe the methods of data collection, presentation, and summarization.
    • Explain measures of central tendency and dispersion.
    • Describe the principles of sampling methods.
    • Explain the concept and application of tests of significance.
    • Describe the principles of correlation and regression analysis.
  16. Health Planning, Management & Health Economics
    • Describe the levels of healthcare delivery in the health system.
    • Explain the principles of health planning and management cycles.
    • Describe the basic concepts of health economics and financing.
    • Explain the concept of universal health coverage.
    • Describe indicators used to evaluate health programme performance.
  17. Hospital Administration & Disaster Management
    • Describe the principles of hospital organization and administration.
    • Explain the concept of hospital waste management.
    • Describe the principles of disaster preparedness and response.
    • Explain the role of public health in disaster and emergency management.
    • Describe the components of a hospital's disaster management plan.
  18. International Health & Global Health Organizations
    • Describe the role and functions of the World Health Organization.
    • Explain the role of international agencies in global health (UNICEF, World Bank).
    • Describe the concept and goals of Sustainable Development Goals related to health.
    • Explain the principles of international health regulations.
    • Describe global initiatives for disease eradication and elimination.

Clinical chapters

Chapter-level breakdown for each clinical subject — use these as the unit of mapping when building competencies above.

General Medicine
  1. Clinical Methods & History Taking
    • Elicit a systematic clinical history from a patient.
    • Perform a general physical examination correctly.
    • Demonstrate correct technique for inspection, palpation, percussion, and auscultation.
    • Formulate a provisional and differential diagnosis based on history and examination.
    • Document clinical findings in a structured case record.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 55-year-old man presents with fatigue. On inspection you note yellowish discoloration of sclera; on palpation the liver edge is felt 3 cm below the costal margin. Point: sequence of clinical method — inspection before palpation guides diagnosis.
    • A medical student takes a history but forgets to ask about drug allergies before a ward round prescription is written. Point: history taking must be systematic to avoid preventable errors.
  2. Cardiovascular Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage common cases of ischemic heart disease.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of heart failure.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of hypertension.
    • Describe the clinical presentation and management of valvular heart disease.
    • Interpret a 12-lead ECG in common cardiac conditions.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 60-year-old diabetic man presents with crushing central chest pain radiating to the left arm, sweating, and ECG shows ST elevation in leads II, III, aVF. Dx: Acute inferior wall myocardial infarction.
    • A 68-year-old woman presents with progressive breathlessness, bilateral pedal edema, and raised JVP with bibasal crepitations. Dx: Congestive cardiac failure.
    • A 45-year-old hypertensive on irregular medication presents with sudden severe tearing chest pain radiating to the back, with unequal blood pressure in both arms. Dx: Aortic dissection.
  3. Respiratory Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage community-acquired pneumonia.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of bronchial asthma and COPD.
    • Explain the diagnosis and treatment of pulmonary tuberculosis.
    • Interpret chest X-rays and pulmonary function tests.
    • Describe the management of respiratory failure.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 30-year-old man presents with fever, cough with rusty sputum, and right-sided pleuritic chest pain; chest X-ray shows right lower lobe consolidation. Dx: Community-acquired pneumonia.
    • A 25-year-old woman has recurrent episodes of wheeze and breathlessness worse at night, relieved by a bronchodilator. Dx: Bronchial asthma.
    • A 40-year-old man with chronic cough, evening rise of temperature, and weight loss has a chest X-ray showing upper lobe cavitary lesion. Dx: Pulmonary tuberculosis.
  4. Gastrointestinal & Hepatobiliary Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage peptic ulcer disease and GI bleeding.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of viral hepatitis.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of liver cirrhosis and its complications.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with jaundice.
    • Explain the management of inflammatory bowel disease.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 50-year-old chronic alcoholic presents with hematemesis, abdominal distension, and spider naevi on the chest wall. Dx: Variceal bleed due to liver cirrhosis with portal hypertension.
    • A 35-year-old man presents with epigastric pain relieved by food, worse at night, with no alarm features. Dx: Duodenal peptic ulcer disease.
    • A 20-year-old presents with fever, malaise, and yellowish discoloration of eyes with dark urine after eating street food. Dx: Acute viral hepatitis A.
  5. Renal Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage acute kidney injury.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of chronic kidney disease.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of nephrotic and nephritic syndrome.
    • Describe the indications for renal replacement therapy.
    • Interpret renal function tests and urine analysis.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 6-year-old child presents with periorbital puffiness, frothy urine, and generalized edema; urine shows heavy proteinuria. Dx: Nephrotic syndrome.
    • A 55-year-old diabetic on NSAIDs for chronic pain presents with reduced urine output and rising creatinine over 3 days. Dx: Acute kidney injury.
    • A 45-year-old man with long-standing diabetes and hypertension presents with fatigue, pallor, and bilateral shrunken kidneys on ultrasound. Dx: Chronic kidney disease.
  6. Endocrine & Metabolic Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage diabetes mellitus and its acute complications.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of thyroid disorders.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of adrenal disorders.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with electrolyte imbalance.
    • Explain the management of metabolic syndrome and obesity.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 22-year-old known type 1 diabetic presents with vomiting, abdominal pain, deep sighing breathing, and fruity breath odor. Dx: Diabetic ketoacidosis.
    • A 35-year-old woman presents with weight loss, heat intolerance, palpitations, and a diffusely enlarged thyroid with fine tremor. Dx: Graves' disease (hyperthyroidism).
    • A 30-year-old woman presents with weight gain, cold intolerance, constipation, and dry skin with delayed relaxation of ankle reflex. Dx: Primary hypothyroidism.
  7. Hematological Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage common types of anemia.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of leukemias.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with bleeding disorders.
    • Describe the indications and complications of blood transfusion.
    • Interpret a complete blood count and peripheral smear.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 20-year-old vegetarian woman presents with fatigue and pallor; peripheral smear shows microcytic hypochromic red cells. Dx: Iron deficiency anemia.
    • A 6-year-old boy presents with fever, easy bruising, and gum bleeding; peripheral smear shows numerous blast cells. Dx: Acute leukemia.
    • A 40-year-old man presents with recurrent epistaxis and prolonged bleeding after minor trauma since childhood, with normal platelet count but prolonged aPTT. Dx: Hemophilia.
  8. Rheumatology & Connective Tissue Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage rheumatoid arthritis.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of systemic lupus erythematosus.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with joint pain and swelling.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of gout.
    • Explain the use of disease-modifying drugs in rheumatologic conditions.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 35-year-old woman presents with symmetrical pain and morning stiffness in small joints of both hands lasting over an hour. Dx: Rheumatoid arthritis.
    • A 25-year-old woman presents with malar rash, photosensitivity, joint pain, and oral ulcers. Dx: Systemic lupus erythematosus.
    • A 50-year-old man presents with sudden severe pain and swelling of the great toe at night after a heavy meat meal. Dx: Acute gouty arthritis.
  9. Neurology
    • Diagnose and manage stroke and transient ischemic attacks.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of epilepsy.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with altered sensorium.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of common headache disorders.
    • Perform and interpret a basic neurological examination.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 65-year-old hypertensive man develops sudden weakness of the left arm and leg with facial droop while gardening. Dx: Acute ischemic stroke.
    • A 19-year-old man has recurrent episodes of sudden loss of consciousness with tonic-clonic movements and tongue bite. Dx: Generalized tonic-clonic seizure/epilepsy.
    • A 40-year-old woman presents with recurrent unilateral throbbing headache with nausea and photophobia, relieved by rest in a dark room. Dx: Migraine.
  10. Infectious Diseases
    • Diagnose and manage common bacterial and viral infections.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of dengue and malaria.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with fever of unknown origin.
    • Describe the principles of rational antimicrobial use.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of HIV/AIDS-related complications.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 25-year-old presents with high-grade fever, retro-orbital pain, myalgia, and a positive tourniquet test with falling platelet count. Dx: Dengue fever.
    • A 30-year-old returning from a rural area presents with cyclical fever with chills and rigors every 48 hours, and peripheral smear shows ring forms. Dx: Malaria (P. vivax).
    • A 45-year-old man presents with fever for 3 weeks without a clear focus despite basic workup. Dx: Pyrexia of unknown origin — needs systematic evaluation.
  11. Nutritional Disorders
    • Assess nutritional status using clinical and anthropometric methods.
    • Describe the clinical features of protein-energy malnutrition in adults.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of vitamin deficiency disorders.
    • Describe the nutritional management of chronic disease.
    • Explain the approach to obesity and metabolic complications.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 50-year-old chronic alcoholic presents with confusion, ataxia, and ophthalmoplegia. Dx: Wernicke's encephalopathy (thiamine deficiency).
    • A 60-year-old vegan presents with numbness in both feet, glossitis, and macrocytic anemia. Dx: Vitamin B12 deficiency.
    • An elderly bedridden patient presents with bleeding gums and perifollicular hemorrhages after months of a fruit-free diet. Dx: Scurvy (Vitamin C deficiency).
  12. Dermatological Manifestations of Systemic Disease
    • Recognize skin signs of common systemic and metabolic diseases.
    • Describe cutaneous manifestations of connective tissue disorders.
    • Explain the skin findings associated with endocrine disorders.
    • Describe drug-induced skin reactions and their management.
    • Correlate skin findings with underlying systemic pathology.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 45-year-old diabetic presents with a well-demarcated, shiny, yellow-brown plaque with telangiectasia over the shin. Dx: Necrobiosis lipoidica diabeticorum.
    • A 30-year-old woman with SLE presents with a butterfly-shaped erythematous rash sparing the nasolabial folds. Point: malar rash is a hallmark cutaneous sign of SLE.
    • A 50-year-old on long-term steroids for a systemic illness presents with new-onset acne and skin thinning. Dx: Drug-induced (steroid) skin changes.
  13. Psychiatric Aspects in Medicine
    • Recognize common psychiatric presentations in medical patients.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with anxiety or depression in a medical setting.
    • Explain the psychiatric aspects of chronic illness.
    • Describe delirium and its differentiation from dementia.
    • Explain when to refer a patient for specialist psychiatric care.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 70-year-old hospitalized patient with a urinary tract infection develops acute fluctuating confusion and disorientation over 2 days. Dx: Delirium secondary to infection.
    • A 55-year-old man newly diagnosed with cancer presents with persistent low mood, poor sleep, and loss of interest for 3 weeks. Point: recognize depression as a comorbid condition needing evaluation in medical illness.
    • A 40-year-old with generalized anxiety presents with palpitations and breathlessness after every doctor's visit, with normal cardiac workup. Point: somatic anxiety symptoms can mimic organic disease.
  14. Medical Emergencies & Critical Care
    • Manage a patient in cardiac arrest using basic and advanced life support.
    • Describe the initial management of shock.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with acute respiratory distress.
    • Describe the management of common poisoning emergencies.
    • Explain the principles of triage in emergency medicine.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 60-year-old man suddenly collapses, is unresponsive, and has no palpable pulse. Point: immediate action is to start chest compressions and call for help (BLS/ACLS).
    • A 30-year-old trauma patient presents with cold clammy skin, tachycardia, and hypotension after a road traffic accident. Dx: Hypovolemic shock.
    • A young woman with severe asthma presents with inability to speak in full sentences, use of accessory muscles, and a silent chest. Dx: Life-threatening acute severe asthma — needs urgent management.
  15. Geriatrics
    • Describe the physiological changes of aging relevant to disease presentation.
    • Explain the approach to polypharmacy in elderly patients.
    • Describe the assessment and management of falls in the elderly.
    • Explain the principles of comprehensive geriatric assessment.
    • Describe common geriatric syndromes and their management.
    Clinical vignettes
    • An 80-year-old woman on 9 different medications presents with recurrent falls and dizziness on standing. Point: polypharmacy and orthostatic hypotension are common causes of falls in the elderly.
    • A 75-year-old man is brought in after a fall at home with a hip fracture; he lives alone and has poor vision. Point: comprehensive geriatric assessment is needed to identify modifiable fall risk factors.
  16. Clinical Toxicology & Snake Bite
    • Describe the clinical features and management of common drug overdoses.
    • Explain the classification and management of snake bite.
    • Describe the use of antivenom and its complications.
    • Explain the general principles of poison management.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with an unknown poisoning.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A farmer is brought with a history of snake bite, ptosis, and difficulty breathing within 2 hours. Dx: Neurotoxic snake envenomation — requires urgent antivenom and airway support.
    • A young woman is brought unconscious with pinpoint pupils and slow respiration after ingesting an unknown substance found beside her. Dx: Suspected opioid poisoning.
    • A farmer presents with excessive salivation, sweating, miosis, and fasciculations after spraying crops without protective gear. Dx: Organophosphorus poisoning.
  17. Immunology & Allergic Disorders
    • Describe the basic mechanisms of hypersensitivity reactions.
    • Diagnose and manage anaphylaxis.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with suspected drug allergy.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of primary immunodeficiency states.
    • Explain the principles of immunosuppressive therapy and its complications.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 25-year-old develops sudden facial swelling, wheeze, and hypotension minutes after a bee sting. Dx: Anaphylaxis — requires immediate intramuscular adrenaline.
    • A 30-year-old develops an itchy rash and fever a week after starting a new antibiotic for a throat infection. Dx: Drug allergy/hypersensitivity reaction.
  18. Environmental & Heat-Related Illness
    • Diagnose and manage heat exhaustion and heat stroke.
    • Describe the clinical features and management of hypothermia.
    • Explain the recognition and management of high-altitude illness.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with near-drowning.
    • Explain the management of electrical and lightning injuries.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A laborer working outdoors in peak summer collapses with a core temperature of 41°C, dry hot skin, and confusion. Dx: Heat stroke — a medical emergency requiring rapid cooling.
    • A trekker at high altitude develops headache, nausea, and breathlessness a day after rapid ascent. Dx: Acute mountain sickness.
  19. Palliative & End-of-Life Care
    • Describe the principles of symptom control in palliative care.
    • Explain the assessment and management of cancer pain.
    • Describe communication skills for breaking bad news.
    • Explain the ethical principles guiding end-of-life care decisions.
    • Describe the role of the multidisciplinary team in palliative care.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 65-year-old with terminal metastatic cancer has severe uncontrolled pain despite oral tramadol. Point: escalate on the WHO analgesic ladder to a strong opioid such as morphine.
    • The family of a terminally ill patient asks the physician to inform the patient there is nothing more wrong, fearing distress from the truth. Point: balancing honest communication and compassionate breaking of bad news.
General Surgery
  1. Surgical Basics — Asepsis, Wound Healing & Sutures
    • Describe the principles of surgical asepsis and sterilization.
    • Explain the phases and factors affecting wound healing.
    • Describe the types of sutures and suturing techniques.
    • Explain the classification and management of surgical site infections.
    • Describe the principles of wound dressing and drain management.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A surgeon inadvertently touches an unsterile surface during a hernia repair; two days later the patient develops purulent wound discharge. Dx: Surgical site infection due to breach of asepsis.
    • A diabetic patient's abdominal wound edges gape open on the 5th postoperative day while coughing. Dx: Wound dehiscence.
  2. Fluid, Electrolyte & Blood Transfusion in Surgery
    • Describe fluid and electrolyte management in the surgical patient.
    • Explain the indications and complications of blood transfusion.
    • Describe the assessment of fluid deficit and replacement therapy.
    • Explain the management of postoperative fluid imbalance.
    • Describe the principles of nutritional support in surgical patients.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient develops fever, chills, and back pain 15 minutes after starting a blood transfusion. Dx: Acute hemolytic transfusion reaction — stop transfusion immediately.
    • A postoperative patient becomes confused and lethargic with a serum sodium of 118 mEq/L after excessive IV fluid replacement. Dx: Dilutional hyponatremia.
  3. Shock, Hemorrhage & Surgical Critical Care
    • Classify and describe the management of hemorrhagic shock.
    • Explain the initial resuscitation of a trauma patient.
    • Describe the principles of surgical critical care and monitoring.
    • Explain the management of sepsis in a surgical patient.
    • Describe the indications for surgical intensive care admission.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A young man after a road accident presents with cold extremities, BP 80/50, pulse 130/min, and pallor. Dx: Hemorrhagic (hypovolemic) shock.
    • A postoperative patient develops high fever, hypotension, and tachycardia with a raised white cell count. Dx: Surgical sepsis — needs source control and antibiotics.
  4. Infections, Ulcers, Sinuses & Fistulae
    • Describe the classification and management of surgical infections.
    • Explain the etiology and management of chronic ulcers.
    • Describe the difference between sinus and fistula and their management.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with a chronic non-healing wound.
    • Describe the management of necrotizing soft tissue infections.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A diabetic patient presents with a foul-smelling, rapidly spreading, crepitant swelling of the leg with pain out of proportion to exam findings. Dx: Necrotizing fasciitis — surgical emergency.
    • A patient has a chronic non-healing ulcer over the medial malleolus with associated varicose veins. Dx: Venous stasis ulcer.
  5. Tumors — General Principles
    • Describe the clinical approach to a patient with a suspected tumor.
    • Explain the principles of tumor staging and grading.
    • Describe the surgical principles of tumor excision.
    • Explain the role of biopsy in tumor diagnosis.
    • Describe the multidisciplinary approach to cancer management.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 55-year-old presents with a hard, fixed, irregular lump; FNAC confirms malignancy. Point: staging investigations are needed before treatment planning.
    • A patient with a suspicious breast lump undergoes triple assessment (clinical exam, imaging, biopsy). Point: illustrates standard oncological diagnostic workup.
  6. Skin, Soft Tissue & Breast Diseases
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of common skin and soft tissue lesions.
    • Explain the clinical evaluation of a breast lump.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of breast cancer.
    • Explain the indications for breast conservation versus mastectomy.
    • Describe the management of soft tissue sarcomas.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 45-year-old woman finds a painless, hard lump in the upper outer quadrant of her breast with skin dimpling. Dx: Carcinoma breast.
    • A young woman has a firm, mobile, painless breast lump that varies with her menstrual cycle. Dx: Fibroadenoma.
  7. Head & Neck Surgery
    • Describe the surgical approach to thyroid swellings.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of neck lumps.
    • Describe the surgical management of salivary gland disorders.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with head and neck malignancy.
    • Describe complications of thyroid and neck surgery.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient develops hoarseness of voice after thyroidectomy. Dx: Recurrent laryngeal nerve injury.
    • A 40-year-old presents with a midline neck swelling that moves upward on protrusion of the tongue. Dx: Thyroglossal cyst.
  8. Esophagus, Stomach & Duodenum
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of gastroesophageal reflux disease.
    • Explain the surgical management of peptic ulcer complications.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of gastric carcinoma.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with dysphagia.
    • Describe the management of upper GI bleeding.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 60-year-old with long-standing GERD presents with progressive dysphagia, first to solids then liquids, with weight loss. Dx: Carcinoma esophagus.
    • A patient with known peptic ulcer disease presents with sudden severe abdominal pain and a rigid, board-like abdomen. Dx: Perforated peptic ulcer.
  9. Liver, Biliary Tract & Pancreas
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of gallstone disease.
    • Explain the surgical approach to obstructive jaundice.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of acute and chronic pancreatitis.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with a liver mass.
    • Describe the surgical management of pancreatic tumors.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 45-year-old obese woman presents with colicky right upper quadrant pain after fatty meals, radiating to the back. Dx: Acute cholecystitis due to gallstones.
    • A 60-year-old presents with painless progressive jaundice and a palpable gallbladder. Dx: Carcinoma head of pancreas (Courvoisier's law).
  10. Small & Large Intestine, Appendix
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of acute appendicitis.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of intestinal obstruction.
    • Describe the surgical approach to colorectal carcinoma.
    • Explain the management of inflammatory bowel disease requiring surgery.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with an acute abdomen.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 20-year-old presents with periumbilical pain migrating to the right iliac fossa, with fever and rebound tenderness. Dx: Acute appendicitis.
    • A 65-year-old presents with colicky abdominal pain, distension, absolute constipation, and vomiting. Dx: Intestinal obstruction.
  11. Anorectal Diseases & Hernias
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of hemorrhoids and fistula-in-ano.
    • Explain the classification and management of abdominal wall hernias.
    • Describe the surgical repair techniques for common hernias.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of anorectal abscess.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with rectal bleeding.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A middle-aged man presents with a painless groin swelling that enlarges on coughing and reduces on lying down. Dx: Inguinal hernia.
    • A patient presents with severe pain during defecation and bright red blood on the stool, with a visible tear at the anal margin. Dx: Anal fissure.
  12. Urology
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of urinary tract calculi.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with urinary retention.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of benign prostatic hyperplasia.
    • Explain the surgical approach to urological malignancies.
    • Describe the management of urinary tract infections requiring surgical intervention.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 60-year-old man presents with poor urinary stream, frequency, and nocturia. Dx: Benign prostatic hyperplasia.
    • A 30-year-old presents with sudden severe colicky loin-to-groin pain with hematuria. Dx: Ureteric calculus (renal colic).
  13. Vascular Surgery
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of peripheral arterial disease.
    • Explain the diagnosis and management of deep vein thrombosis.
    • Describe the approach to a patient with varicose veins.
    • Explain the management of vascular trauma.
    • Describe the indications for vascular surgical intervention.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 60-year-old smoker presents with cramping calf pain on walking that resolves with rest. Dx: Intermittent claudication due to peripheral arterial disease.
    • A postoperative bedridden patient develops sudden unilateral leg swelling and calf tenderness. Dx: Deep vein thrombosis.
  14. Neurosurgery — Basics
    • Describe the initial assessment of a patient with head injury.
    • Explain the indications for surgical intervention in traumatic brain injury.
    • Describe the clinical features of raised intracranial pressure.
    • Explain the approach to a patient with spinal cord compression.
    • Describe the basic principles of neurosurgical emergencies.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient after a head injury has a lucid interval followed by deteriorating consciousness and a dilated pupil on one side. Dx: Extradural hematoma.
    • An elderly patient on anticoagulants presents with gradual headache and confusion weeks after a minor fall. Dx: Chronic subdural hematoma.
  15. Thoracic & Cardiovascular Surgery Basics
    • Describe the management of chest trauma.
    • Explain the indications for chest tube insertion.
    • Describe the basic surgical approach to lung and mediastinal masses.
    • Explain the principles of cardiac surgery for common conditions.
    • Describe the postoperative care of thoracic surgical patients.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A trauma patient has absent breath sounds on one side with hyper-resonance and tracheal deviation away from the affected side. Dx: Tension pneumothorax — needs immediate needle decompression.
    • A stab injury patient presents with muffled heart sounds, distended neck veins, and hypotension. Dx: Cardiac tamponade (Beck's triad).
  16. Pediatric Surgery
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of common congenital surgical anomalies.
    • Explain the surgical approach to pediatric hernias and hydrocele.
    • Describe the management of pyloric stenosis.
    • Explain the approach to a neonate with intestinal obstruction.
    • Describe the principles of perioperative care in pediatric surgical patients.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A 3-week-old male infant presents with non-bilious projectile vomiting after feeds and a palpable olive-shaped epigastric mass. Dx: Hypertrophic pyloric stenosis.
    • A newborn presents with bilious vomiting and abdominal distension within the first day of life. Dx: Intestinal obstruction (e.g., duodenal/jejunal atresia).
  17. Trauma & Burns
    • Describe the primary and secondary survey in trauma assessment.
    • Explain the classification and initial management of burns.
    • Describe the management of polytrauma patients.
    • Explain fluid resuscitation formulas in burn management.
    • Describe the principles of damage control surgery.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient with 40% total body surface area burns has IV fluid resuscitation calculated using the Parkland formula for the first 24 hours. Point: illustrates burns fluid resuscitation.
    • A polytrauma patient is assessed using the ABCDE primary survey approach on arrival in the emergency department. Point: illustrates systematic trauma assessment.
  18. Perioperative Care & Surgical Techniques
    • Describe the components of preoperative assessment and optimization.
    • Explain the principles of informed consent for surgery.
    • Describe common surgical instruments and their uses.
    • Explain the principles of minimally invasive surgery.
    • Describe the postoperative complications and their management.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient develops fever and reduced breath sounds at the lung base on the first postoperative day. Dx: Atelectasis — most common cause of early postoperative fever.
    • A patient develops calf pain and swelling on the 4th postoperative day after major abdominal surgery. Dx: Deep vein thrombosis.
  19. Lymphatic Disorders
    • Describe the causes and classification of lymphedema.
    • Explain the clinical approach to a patient with lymphadenopathy.
    • Describe the diagnosis and management of filarial lymphedema.
    • Explain the surgical and conservative management options for lymphedema.
    • Describe the complications of chronic lymphatic obstruction.
    Clinical vignettes
    • A patient from an endemic area presents with massive swelling of one lower limb with thickened skin. Dx: Filarial lymphedema.
